def vm():
    out = subprocess.run(["vm_stat"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    d = {m.group(1).strip(): int(m.group(2)) * 16384 / 1e9 for m in re.finditer(r"(.+?):\s+(\d+)\.", out)}
    return {k: d[k] for k in ("Pages free", "Anonymous pages", "File-backed pages", "Pages wired down")}
def kern(path):
    b = open(path, "rb").read(65536); ncmds = struct.unpack_from("<I", b, 16)[0]; off = 32
    for _ in range(ncmds):

            _, _, fo, fs = struct.unpack_from("<4Q", b, off+24); return fo, fs
        off += size

from coreai_bench_helpers import SEED, remove_path, specialization_for, to_numpy

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ARTIFACTS = ROOT / "artifacts_vector_lut"
CACHE = Path.home() / "Library/Caches/coreai-cache"

VARIANTS = {  # name: (cluster_dim, n_bits); None = dense FP16
    "dense": None,
    "s8": (1, 8),
    "s4": (1, 4),
    "s2": (1, 2),
    "v2n4": (2, 4),
    "v4n4": (4, 4),
    "v4n6": (4, 6),
    "v2n6": (2, 6),
    "v8n4": (8, 4),
    "v8n2": (8, 2),
    "v16n4": (16, 4),
    "v2n8": (2, 8),  # 512 LUT values: over the ANE's 256-value limit
}

# Exact LUT-generated weights: hand the pass the LUT and indices each layer was
# built from (keyed by the FP16 weight bytes); fall back to UNIQUE mode.
_orig_blockwise_compress = weight_palettization._blockwise_compress
KNOWN_LUTS: dict[bytes, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}


def _unique_blockwise_compress(original_data, mode, *args, **kwargs):
    known = KNOWN_LUTS.get(np.ascontiguousarray(original_data, np.float16).tobytes())
    block_sizes = args[1] if len(args) > 1 else kwargs.get("block_sizes")
    per_tensor = not block_sizes or all(b in (0, n) for b, n in zip(block_sizes, original_data.shape))
    if known is not None and per_tensor:
        lut, idx = known
        cluster_dim = lut.shape[1]
        extra = (1,) * (original_data.ndim - 2)
        return LutParams(
            indices=idx.reshape(*idx.shape, *extra).astype(np.uint8),
            lut=lut.reshape(*(1,) * original_data.ndim, *lut.shape),
            vector_axis=0 if cluster_dim > 1 else None,
        )
    return _orig_blockwise_compress(original_data, "UNIQUE", *args, **kwargs)


weight_palettization._blockwise_compress = _unique_blockwise_compress


def _groups1_cluster_dim_valid(op, cluster_dim: int, channel_axis: int) -> bool:
    # coreai-opt 0.2.1's check reads conv2d `.groups`, which the OpView lacks
    # (AttributeError). All convs here are groups=1, so the shape test suffices.
    shape = list(op.result.type.shape)
    return shape[channel_axis] % cluster_dim == 0


weight_palettization._is_cluster_dim_valid = _groups1_cluster_dim_valid


class ConvChain(nn.Module):
    """S layers of 1x1 Conv2d(C, C), or nn.Linear(C, C) with op="linear"."""

    def __init__(self, c: int, stack: int, op: str = "conv") -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.op = op
        make = (lambda: nn.Conv2d(c, c, 1, bias=False)) if op == "conv" else (
            lambda: nn.Linear(c, c, bias=False))
        self.convs = nn.ModuleList(make() for _ in range(stack))

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        for conv in self.convs:
            x = conv(x)
        return x


def lut_weight(lut: np.ndarray, idx: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """(C_out, C_in) weight with w[o*cd + j, i] = lut[idx[o, i], j]."""
    n_out, c_in = idx.shape
    return lut[idx].transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(n_out * lut.shape[1], c_in)


def input_shape(c: int, hw: int, op: str) -> tuple[int, ...]:
    return (1, c, hw, hw) if op == "conv" else (hw * hw, c)


def build_model(
    c: int, hw: int, stack: int, cfg: tuple[int, int] | None, op: str = "conv"
) -> ConvChain:
    """Chain whose every layer is generated from one random LUT.

    Each LUT is rescaled (a scalar, so the LUT structure stays exact) so that
    the benchmark input keeps unit RMS through every layer; unscaled random
    chains drift far enough over 32 layers to overflow FP16.
    """
    torch.manual_seed(SEED)
    model = ConvChain(c, stack, op).eval()
    rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)
    cluster_dim, n_bits = cfg or (1, 8)  # dense: 256-level weights, stored FP16
    torch.manual_seed(SEED)
    x = torch.randn(*input_shape(c, hw, op)).numpy().astype(np.float64)
    x = x.reshape(c, -1) if op == "conv" else x.T
    with torch.no_grad():
        for conv in model.convs:
            lut = rng.standard_normal((1 << n_bits, cluster_dim)) * c**-0.5
            idx = rng.integers(0, 1 << n_bits, size=(c // cluster_dim, c))
            y = lut_weight(lut, idx) @ x
            lut = (lut / np.sqrt((y**2).mean())).astype(np.float16)
            w = lut_weight(lut, idx).astype(np.float32)
            KNOWN_LUTS[w.astype(np.float16).tobytes()] = (lut, idx)
            conv.weight.copy_(torch.from_numpy(w).view_as(conv.weight))
            x = w.astype(np.float64) @ x
    return model


def export(
    name: str, c: int, hw: int, stack: int, force: bool, op: str = "conv", group_size: int = 0,
    lut_dtype: str = "fp16",
) -> tuple[Path, ConvChain, str]:
    cfg = VARIANTS[name]
    tag = ("" if op == "conv" else f"_{op}") + (f"_G{group_size}" if group_size else "")
    tag += "" if lut_dtype == "fp16" else f"_{lut_dtype}"
    out = ARTIFACTS / f"{name}_C{c}_HW{hw}_S{stack}{tag}.aimodel"
    ir_path = out.with_suffix(".mlir")
    model = build_model(c, hw, stack, cfg, op)
    if out.exists() and ir_path.exists() and not force:
        return out, model, ir_path.read_text()
    example = torch.randn(*input_shape(c, hw, op))
    exported = torch.export.export(
        model.to(torch.float16), (example.to(torch.float16),), strict=False
    ).run_decompositions(coreai_torch.get_decomp_table())
    model.float()
    cast_to_16_bit_precision(exported)
    converter = coreai_torch.TorchConverter(mode=coreai_torch.TorchConverter.Mode.RELEASE)
    converter.add_exported_program(exported, input_names=["x"], output_names=["y"])
    program = converter.to_coreai()
    program.optimize()
    if cfg is not None:
        cluster_dim, n_bits = cfg
        program = palettize_weights(
            program,
            lut_dtype={"fp16": None, "int8": DType.INT8, "fp8": DType.FP8_E4M3FN}[lut_dtype],
            n_bits=n_bits,
            granularity=(CompressionGranularity.PER_GROUPED_CHANNEL if group_size
                         else CompressionGranularity.PER_TENSOR),
            group_size=group_size or 32,
            cluster_dim=cluster_dim,
            weight_num_threshold=1024,
            enable_fast_kmeans_mode=False,
        )
    ir = str(program)
    ARTIFACTS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    remove_path(out)
    program.save_asset(out)
    ir_path.write_text(ir)
    return out, model, ir


def ir_summary(ir: str) -> str:
    luts = re.findall(r"coreai\.lut_to_dense[^\n]*", ir)
    if not luts:
        return "no lut_to_dense (dense weights)"
    shapes = sorted(set(re.findall(r"tensor<(\d+x\d+x\d+x\d+x\d+x?f16)>", " ".join(luts))))
    return f"{len(luts)}x lut_to_dense, LUT {shapes[:1]}"


def placement(path: Path) -> tuple[str, float]:
    """(placement flags, compiled package MB) from the newest cache entry of this model."""
    digest = (path / "main.hash").read_bytes().hex()
    manifests = sorted(
        CACHE.glob(f"*/*/{digest}/*/model.aimodelx/**/manifest.plist"),
        key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime,
    )
    if not manifests:
        return "no cache entry", 0.0
    man = manifests[-1]
    text = man.read_bytes()
    flags = [f for f in ("mps.fullyPlacedOnANE", "mps.noGPUActivity") if f.encode() in text]
    if b"ANE_region" in text and b"mps.fullyPlacedOnANE" not in text:
        flags.append("partial ANE region")
    elif b"ANE_region" not in text:
        flags.append("NO ANE region")
    pkg = man.parent
    mb = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in pkg.rglob("*") if f.is_file()) / 1e6
    try:
        plistlib.loads(text)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return ", ".join(flags), mb

    fn = model.load_function("main")
    name = list(fn.desc.input_names)[0]

    for _ in range(warmup):
        out = await fn(inputs={name: nd})
    times = []
    for _ in range(iters):
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        out = await fn(inputs={name: nd})
        times.append(time.perf_counter() - t0)
    y = to_numpy(list(out.values())[0] if isinstance(out, dict

// Attention-shaped matmul throughput on the GPU matrix units (MPP matmul2d): per KV head, scores = Q (384 rows =
// 6 q heads x 64 tokens, hd 256) x K^T (hd 256 x L keys, keys stored dim x token as in the kv8 cache). One
// threadgroup = 64 rows x 64 keys over k = 256; result reduced in registers (stands in for the on-chip softmax).
// fp16 x fp16 -> f32 and int8 x int8 -> int32. PV has the same FLOPs.
import Foundation
import Metal
let dev = MTLCreateSystemDefaultDevice()!
let H = 4, R = 384, D = 256
func run(_ L: Int, _ int8: Bool) {
    let es = int8 ? 1 : 2
    let Q = dev.makeBuffer(length: H * R * D * es, options: .storageModeShared)!, K = dev.makeBuffer(length: H * D * L * es, options: .storageModeShared)!
    let O = dev.makeBuffer(length: H * (R / 64) * (L / 64) * 4, options: .storageModeShared)!
    let qb = Q.contents().bindMemory(to: UInt8.self, capacity: Q.length), kb = K.contents().bindMemory(to: UInt8.self, capacity: K.length)
    for i in 0..<Q.length { qb[i] = int8 ? UInt8(truncatingIfNeeded: (i * 37) % 200 + 28) : (i % 2 == 0 ? UInt8(i % 251) : 0x30) }
    for i in 0..<K.length { kb[i] = int8 ? UInt8(truncatingIfNeeded: (i * 53) % 200 + 28) : (i % 2 == 0 ? UInt8(i % 241) : 0x2c) }
    let t = int8 ? "int8_t" : "half", acc = int8 ? "int32_t" : "float"
    let src = """
    #

53:The MLP at 64 rows runs at about 24 TFLOPS of FP16 work, close to compute bound. The GDN core costs as much as an MLP layer at 64 rows with almost no arithmetic: it was op and latency bound.
227:**First start guidance.** The runtime (and `python forge.py compile --build <dir>`) prints `[ANE compile]` lines from [`scripts/coreai_compile_guide.py`](../../scripts/coreai_compile_guide.py): how many packages are not yet compiled for this macOS build and Python, an estimate from the build's tile settings (fit to the table above, then re-scaled by measured package times), a heartbeat with the time left every 30 s, and only the build options that would compile faster. A cold package compiles in a worker thread, so Ctrl-C exits at once; finished packages stay cached and the next start resumes. `scripts/qwen38_server.sh start` shows these lines live and Ctrl-C there only stops watching.
307:| 256 | 13.3 TOPS | 34.2 (conv) / 31.3 (matmul) | 34.4 (conv) | 53.2 (matmul) |
310:- **INT8 x INT8 engages in Core AI on the M6 ANE**, for constant weights and for a runtime second operand. The ceiling is about **53 TOPS**: A8A8 stays there from 256 to 1,024 rows (a compute limit, not bandwidth), the FP8 peak measured earlier. Constant-weight W8A8 reaches it with a **shared weight scale** (2.8x FP16 at 1,024 rows); per-channel scales cost about 28%.
311:- **Weights must be compile-time INT8 constants:** the `quantize_weights` pass, or `coreai.constexpr_blockwise_shift_scale` written in torch, both lowering to `coreai.blockwise_shift_scale` on a constant that feeds the conv or matmul directly (a reshape in between makes the pass skip it). A runtime `coreai.dequantize` of a constant is rejected by ANEC for conv. INT8 weights with FP16 activations: 23.0 TOPS.
312:- **Output-channel splits** with separate constants per branch help at 256 rows (shared scale: 34.4 to 43.3 TOPS with TP2, 40.7 with TP4) and not at 1,024 (already at the ceiling); FP16 is unchanged. The 1 MiB-per-core weight window (K 4096 against 4032) moved matmul about 9% and conv not at all.
626:  The only form that fits beside the LUT weights in 32 GB (LUT weights times INT8 activations) does not compile for the ANE. Dense W8A8 runs on the ANE but is weight-bandwidth bound even at 64 rows (about 147 GB/s) and slower than the release LUT path, which already runs the 64-row MLP at about 22.5 TFLOPS. A first attempt of this probe failed with `ANEProgramProcessRequestDirect() ... Request cancelled` while another job held the ANE; the rerun on an idle ANE is the table above.

$ cat /Users/anemll/.claude/projects/-Users-anemll-SourceRelease-GITHUB-ML-playground-anemll-forge/memory/ane-hwx-inspection.md; strings /System/Library/PrivateFrameworks/AppleNeuralEngine.framework/Versions/A/AppleNeuralEngine 2>/dev/null | grep -E "^kANEF[A-Za-z]*(Type|HWX|Precompiled|Model)" | sort -u | head -40

name: ane-hwx-inspection

metadata:

Compiled ANE programs live in aned's cache, not the Core AI cache (that one holds only MLIR with ANE regions).
Mapping: package main.hash -> ~/Library/Caches/coreai-cache/<build>/python/<hex>/*/model.aimodelx/**/manifest.plist
-> "ANERegionsHash" {h18g: "<a>_<b>"} -> /Library/Caches/com.apple.aned/<build>/ModelAssetsCache/-_unsigned/<a>/<b>/model.hwx.

- Read access: the user granted a read ACL (inherited) on /Library/Caches/com.apple.aned and com.apple.aneuserd on
  4 Oct 2026 via an admin prompt; the user must approve root steps themselves (auto mode blocks permission changes).
  Both dirs have the `datavault` flag: reading them needs SIP disabled (it is on this M6) as well as the ACL.
  The user cleaned aned's cache with sudo on 5 Oct; dangling Core AI entries then give nilError until moved aside.
- Guide for agents: docs/ANE_HWX_MLIR_ANALYSIS.md (workflow, fusion rules, validation ladder, debugging recipe).
- Parser: hwx_parsing from github.com/freedomtan/coreml_to_ane_hwx (built in a session scratchpad; rebuild with make
  in hwx_dump/ if gone). Supports h18g (M6, subtype 0xb, ISA v24).
- Repo tool: scripts/m6_hwx_inspect.py (map / summary --ne-dims / pseudo), HWX_PARSING env points at the parser.
- Signatures: true INT8 x INT8 = NE task in=int8 kern=int8 out=int8; vector LUT = KernelCfg Fmt=fp16 Pal=1(4bit);
  ExeCycles are static compiler estimates, not measurements.

- 13 Nov 2026: aned's cache dir was recreated 27 Oct, which dropped the ACL; the user re-granted it (chmod -R +a
  "user:anemll allow list,search,read,...,file_inherit,directory_inherit"). Check with `ls -le /Library/Caches`.
- HWX is Mach-O shaped (magic 0xbeefface, LC_SEGMENT_64): weights in `__KERN_0/__kern_0` (632 MB for a 4-layer
  release chunk), 16 KB aligned in the file.
- Vector-LUT (cd 2) weight layout, DECODED 13 Nov 2026 (controlled probe + all 6 palettized matrices of layer 0,
  GPU GEMV from the HWX == export lut[idx]*scale to fp32 rounding): 32 blocks per matrix = 2 halves x 16 NE blocks;
  block = [palette verbatim, 2^bits x 2 fp16] then subgroups [2G fp16 per-channel scales][G index rows x Cin,
  column-major (per input column G indices), LSB-first `bits`-bit]. G = 16 rows, but 8 for in_proj_qkv. Half h,
  block b, subgroup s -> index rows h*R/2 + G*b + 16*G*s; channel 2r+j = lut[idx][j]. The stored half order varies
  per matrix (usually second half first): detect it (one row) or read the task descriptors. Scales are the export's,
  except the gate's: x0.5 (tanh-SiLU fold). Blocks of gate / up interleave per half (gate A, up A, gate B, up B).
  GPU GEMM T=64 from the HWX: Metal 4 tensor ops (MPP matmul2d, TM 128 x 64, 4 simdgroups, threadgroup-decoded
  weights) 9-12 TFLOPS, rel err ~3e-6; simdgroup_matrix tops out at 4.8. Six projections of a DeltaNet layer:
  4.2 ms alone, 5.36 ms while the ANE prefills (-28%); ANE prefill 309 -> 297 tok/s under full GPU load (-4%).
  One snapshot after ~11 s ANE idle showed wired 18.9 -> 3.7 GB (pages still file-cached; seen once, unconfirmed). Driver: freeze_kv_slice_v1/gpu_ane_overlap.py.
  Not decoded yet: FP8 attention weights and dense FP16 (lr_a/lr_b, in_proj_a/b). Tools: scratchpad hwx_probe/
  (gpu_check.py, gpu/hwx_lut_gemv.swift, hwx_lut_probe.py) of session e4e29b3c.
- The ANE runs from the page-cache pages of model.hwx, wired while loaded (+12.6 GB wired, no anonymous copy; on
  unload they turn back into file cache). A Metal buffer made with bytesNoCopy over an mmap of __KERN reads the same
  pages (GPU sum == CPU, 144 GB/s, no memory growth). ANE prefill 311 -> 253 tok/s while the GPU streams at full
  bandwidth. Tools: scratchpad hwx_gpu/ (session e4e29b3c), C/.../freeze_kv_slice_v1/hwx_share_check.py.

---
name: gpu-ane-prefill-variants
description: "measured GPU+ANE prefill variants on the M6 (13 Nov 2026): layer pipeline 1.70x at 8K, attention offload strong at 64K, MLP/tensor splits rejected"
metadata:
  node_type: memory
  type: project
  originSessionId: e4e29b3c-8360-4ddd-8f48-0762d3ef8250
  modified: 2026-11-13T19:39:53.799Z
---

Measured 13 Nov 2026 on the segmented 96K build (coreai_seg2), 64-token blocks, chunk plans only (no head/host):
- V1 layer pipeline (ANE runs chunks 0..c-1 of block b+1, GPU runs the other 4(16-c) layers of block b from the
  ANE's own HWX weights): 8K best c=9: 199 -> 116.5 ms per block (322 -> 549 tok/s, 1.70x). ANE slows only 1-5%
  under GPU load, GPU ~15% under ANE load (3.3 -> 3.8 ms per DeltaNet layer). 64K: 338 -> 177 ms at c=8 (1.9x) but the
  GPU proxy lacked the FA attention core (add >= 3.1 ms per GPU FA layer at 64K, int8). No new ANE builds needed;

----- output
On a full chunk (chunk 0, `kv8`, interleaved A/B) the gain mostly disappears: `split` for prefill only gives +0.7%, +0.4% and -3.4% at 8K, 32K and 64K with verify bit-identical; `split` everywhere adds +0.2 to +1.5% to verify; `online` everywhere is within about 1% both ways. A control that recomputes the scores timed the same as two-pass: the compiler merges the duplicate. With 128-row prefill calls the forms still differ by about 1%. The two-pass softmax is not a bottleneck.

### Larger prefill calls

Chunk 0, `kv8`, 128-row prefill entries (`TPS=128`): 36.3, 46.1 and 60.1 ms at 8K, 32K and 64K against 12.5, 17.7 and 24.8 ms for 64 rows, so 45%, 30% and 21% slower per row. The 64-row call is already the efficient size on the ANE.

### INT8 compute on the ANE

Localized probe ([`scripts/m6_int8_probe.py`](../../scripts/m6_int8_probe.py)): one op type, 8 layers with distinct weights, 4096 x 4096, dense random data that neither clips nor contains runs of zeros, native `coreai.quantize` / `dequantize` with scales on both operands and the output. Everything below ran fully on the ANE.

| Rows per call | FP16 | W8A8, per-channel weight scale | W8A8, shared weight scale | A8A8, runtime INT8 operand |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 256 | 13.3 TOPS | 34.2 (conv) / 31.3 (matmul) | 34.4 (conv) | 53.2 (matmul) |
| 1,024 | 18.6 (conv) | 38.4 (conv) | **52.8 (conv)** | 53.2 (matmul) |

- **INT8 x INT8 engages in Core AI on the M6 ANE**, for constant weights and for a runtime second operand. The ceiling is about **53 TOPS**: A8A8 stays there from 256 to 1,024 rows (a compute limit, not bandwidth), the FP8 peak measured earlier. Constant-weight W8A8 reaches it with a **shared weight scale** (2.8x FP16 at 1,024 rows); per-channel scales cost about 28%.
- **Weights must be compile-time INT8 constants:** the `quantize_weights` pass, or `coreai.constexpr_blockwise_shift_scale` written in torch, both lowering to `coreai.blockwise_shift_scale` on a constant that feeds the conv or matmul directly (a reshape in between makes the pass skip it). A runtime `coreai.dequantize` of a constant is rejected by ANEC for conv. INT8 weights with FP16 activations: 23.0 TOPS.
- **Output-channel splits** with separate constants per branch help at 256 rows (shared scale: 34.4 to 43.3 TOPS with TP2, 40.7 with TP4) and not at 1,024 (already at the ceiling); FP16 is unchanged. The 1 MiB-per-core weight window (K 4096 against 4032) moved matmul about 9% and conv not at all.
- **Zeros:** 90% zeros in the inputs ran 2 to 13% faster, so timing data must be dense. mactop's per-cluster ANE fields read 100% even at idle, so cluster use could not be observed; ANE bandwidth during these runs was 113 to 128 GB/s.

Applied to the history attention (research switch `ATT_INT8MM`, one `kv8` layer, wrong numerics accepted): INT8 QK with the keys as the untransposed operand and a requantized output cut verify by 13 to 15%; INT8 QK and PV with requantized outputs cut the core by 12 to 17%. A timing control that replaces both history matmuls with reductions reading every key and value (`nomm`) removes only 7 to 10% of prefill and nothing from verify: **the matmuls are under a tenth of the attention core**. The INT8 variants saved more than that because requantized scores and weights shrink the score-sized intermediates (and the PV variants zeroed many small weights). The long-context lever is the bytes and passes over the score tensors (scaling, mask, max, exp, sum), not multiply-add speed.

Where INT8 x INT8 can matter is the weight matmuls of prefill, which are compute-bound. INT8 weights for the whole model (about 27 GB) do not fit beside the rest on 32 GB, so the candidate is our 2/4-bit LUT weights with INT8 activations, built the way that works here (compile-time weights, shared scales, output splits) and measured at the 64-row prefill size. An earlier LUT x INT8-activation probe that failed ANEC predates these corrections.

## Follow-up, 5 October: compiled ANE programs and 8-bit attention (Measured)

......
| `minval` mode | Takes the no-fusion path, like zero point -128 |
| Per-axis scales | Constant scales only; a runtime per-row scale fails ANEC |
| int4 / uint4 activations | Too coarse for scores or weights |
| `lut_to_dense`, `constexpr_blockwise_shift_scale` (including FP4 e2m1 block-scaled data), `sparse_to_dense` | Weights only |

### Next

- `s8b` (step 1/4) + `pvtu` on one chunk, and with `sm8` on M6, A/B at 8K to 64K, then a full build and KL-512.
- Value (and key) scales per tile instead of per token: removes the fold and key-scale passes and most of the `pvtu` error.
- A transposed key cache: removes the per-tile key transposes (19% of verify).
- Retrain the rank-64 corrections with the 8-bit attention simulated if KL-512 moves.

## Rejected or not pursued

- **Kronecker Hadamard** (`H_1024 = H_32 x H_32`, two 32 x 32 grouped convs and a channel transpose): exact but slower than the release 1-bit grouped conv (0.47 versus 0.39 ms for four 17408-channel rotations at T=8).
- **Core AI composite ops.** `coreai_torch` ships `GatedDeltaUpdate` and `SDPA` composites. The DeltaNet composite is an FP32 while-loop over tokens; Apple's authoring notes place native SDPA on the GPU path and recommend per-head attention on the ANE. Not tested further.
- **INT8 / FP8 attention MACs.** Built and measured on 5 October (above): only the history PV pays, and half of that gain is the 1-byte probability write, not the narrower MAC. The history path stays dominated by elementwise passes over scores.
- **W8A8 for the MLP.** Closed for this model with the current toolchain (Measured). Four 1x1 convs 5120 to 17408 to 5120 (357M weights), Core AI, idle ANE:

  | Weights x activations | Placement | T=8 | T=64 |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | FP16 dense x FP16 | ANE | 6.23 ms | 7.26 ms |
  | INT8 dense x INT8 (coreai-opt `Quantizer`, per-channel weights, per-tensor activations) | ANE | 2.42 ms | 2.41 ms |
  | 2-bit vector LUT (release form) x FP16 | ANE | 0.81 ms | 2.03 ms |
  | 2-bit vector LUT with INT8 values x INT8 | ANEC compile fails, GPU | 26.0 ms | 26.5 ms |

  The only form that fits beside the LUT weights in 32 GB (LUT weights times INT8 activations) does not compile for the ANE. Dense W8A8 runs on the ANE but is weight-bandwidth bound even at 64 rows (about 147 GB/s) and slower than the release LUT path, which already runs the 64-row MLP at about 22.5 TFLOPS. A first attempt of this probe failed with `ANEProgramProcessRequestDirect() ... Request cancelled` while another job held the ANE; the rerun on an idle ANE is the table above.

CAI=<Core AI conversion venv>/bin/python     # coreai-torch 0.4.2, coreai-opt 0.2.1, torch 2.11
python scripts/m6_entry_sweep.py --build <build> --format v8 --chunks all --chain
$CAI scripts/m6_gdn_bench.py check --variants ref,fast_b
$CAI scripts/m6_gdn_bench.py build --variants ref,fast_b --out DIR && $CAI scripts/m6_gdn_bench.py time --variants ref,fast_b --out DIR
$CAI scripts/m6_attn_bench.py build --variants ref,b2k --ctx 65472 --out DIR && $CAI scripts/m6_attn_bench.py time --variants ref,b2k --ctx 65472 --out DIR
EXPORT_DIR=<export> GDN_FAST=1 ATT_BLOCK=2048 $CAI coreai/qwen38_coreai_build.py all --kv-cache-dtype both \
  --ctx 8192,16384,32768,49152,65536 --pctx 8192,16384,32768,49152,65536

## Summary

The server's prompt snapshots ("system" / "turn") live only in RAM. A server restart, or a second conversation that overwrites the live KV rows, means the next request on a long conversation prefills it again: about 5 minutes at 64K tokens and 13 minutes at 126K tokens on an M6 Mac mini. I wrote an opt-in disk tier that saves the turn snapshot together with its KV rows and drafter ring, and restores it when a later prompt starts with the same tokens. Restore takes 1 to 2 seconds and gives the same logits as the in-memory restore.

Per CONTRIBUTING.md I'm opening an issue before a PR, to ask whether you want this in Forge and in what shape. The code is on my fork: [SA4DRAO/anemll-forge `feat/prompt-disk-cache`](https://github.com/SA4DRAO/anemll-forge/tree/feat/prompt-disk-cache), based on `main` 703131e.

The technique itself is not new. vLLM with LMCache and SGLang's HiCache have disk and host tiers, and their support for hybrid DeltaNet/attention models is still experimental. What is specific here is the Forge runtime: the ANE's slow prefill makes the gain large, and the restore is exact for both kinds of state.

## Design

- Enabled only when `PROMPT_DISK_DIR` is set. Other variables: `PROMPT_DISK_GB` (16), `PROMPT_DISK_MIN_GAIN` (2048), `PROMPT_DISK_SAVE_GAP` (8192).
- On the turn snapshot, the server writes the KV rows `[0, pos)` (about 33 KB per token with kv8), the DeltaNet states (101 MB), the DFlash2 ring (42 MB), the drafter's slot map and pending rows (`.npz`), and the token ids.
- Before choosing a prefill path, the server uses the longest saved prefix that covers at least `MIN_GAIN` more tokens than memory offers.
- Writes go to `.tmp`, are fsynced, then renamed. Entries are tagged with the build `manifest.json` hash, the KV format, the key layout, the drafter `main.hash`, and ctx.
- A longer entry replaces its prefixes. The size cap evicts least recently used first. Reads use `F_NOCACHE`.
- A failed save or load is logged and the request is prefilled normally. Unfinished saves are removed at startup.
- A restore discards the in-memory snapshots of other prompts, because it overwrites the live KV rows. Without this, an old "system" snapshot could be restored onto another conversation's KV; I reproduced that as wrong output.
- Code: `scripts/prompt_disk_cache.py` (new) and three hooks in `scripts/qwen38_server.py`, about 30 lines. Only the batched-prefill (Core AI) path is supported; on the Core ML path the variable is ignored with a log line.

## Measurements

The setup was an M6 Mac mini with 32 GB, macOS 27.2, Python 3.11.18, numpy 2.4.6 and coreai-core 1.0.0b3. The build was release 0.2.1 at ctx 131072 with the kv8 cache and DFlash2 `dflash2_lut4_gptq_nohead`. Entries were stored on the internal SSD.

Method:
- `scripts/bench_prompt_disk.py` uses `qwen38_server.Engine`, the server's own code.
- The prompt is synthetic: the repo's own docs tiled to N tokens.
- The cold run prefills the prompt and saves the entry. It then runs a follow-up that shares the prefix from the in-memory snapshot; this is the reference.
- The warm run happens in a new process. It re-clones the entry files with `cp -c` so the reads miss the page cache, and runs the same follow-up from disk.
- Generation is greedy, 64 tokens, with thinking off.
- One sample per size. The prompts are 8,026, 64,026 and 126,026 tokens. The build's context is 128K (131,072 tokens); the largest prompt stays below it so the follow-up (about 20 tokens) and the 64 generated tokens fit. The table is from the 2026-11-03 run. A run on 2026-11-02 with the earlier code gave restores of 0.16, 0.89 and 1.61 s, and first tokens after the disk restore of 0.61, 1.50 and 2.36 s.

| prompt | cold prefill | save (entry size) | restore | first token after disk restore | first token after in-memory restore | logits vs in-memory restore |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8K | 28 s | 0.06 s (0.4 GB) | 0.14 s | 0.63 s | 0.34 s | identical (max diff 0.0), same 64 tokens |
| 64K | 300 s | 0.74 s (2.26 GB) | 0.91 s | 1.58 s | 0.50 s | identical (max diff 0.0), same 64 tokens |
| 126K | 785 s | 1.58 s (4.30 GB) | 1.81 s | 3.10 s | 0.52 s | identical (max diff 0.0), same 64 tokens |

Notes:
- A cold prefill of the follow-up is not a valid reference, because different block boundaries change fp16 rounding: the logits differ by about 3.4 with the same top token. The comparison is therefore against the in-memory restore.
- The first token after a disk restore arrives 0.3 s (8K), 1.1 s (64K) and 2.6 s (126K) later than after an in-memory restore. The extra time varies between runs: the first token at 126K was 2.36 s on 2026-11-02 and 3.10 s on 2026-11-03. I haven't found the cause.
- Restore reads at about 2.4 to 2.5 GB/s at 64K and 126K in this run (about 2.7 GB/s on 2026-11-02). An external USB drive would be much slower.
- I did not measure memory peaks; the read path allocates one array-sized buffer at a time.
- I measured through `Engine`, not through HTTP.

## Tests

- `tests/test_prompt_disk_cache.py` has 10 host tests:
  - save/load round trip
  - prefix and min-gain lookup
  - fingerprint mismatch
  - supersede
  - save gap
  - eviction
  - `.tmp` cleanup
  - failed save
  - fingerprint by content
  - folder mode
- `scripts/check_prompt_disk.py` runs 15 checks on the real model and all pass:
  - alternating conversations restore exactly
  - the stale-snapshot case, including a reproduction of the old behaviour

// GPU read bandwidth on this machine: a 16-byte-vector sum over buffers of the decode KV's size (int8 K + V of 16
// layers at N keys = 16 x 4 x N x 256 x 2 bytes), 4 distinct 400 MB buffers cycled; GB/s by GPU time.
import Foundation
import Metal

let src = """
#include <metal_stdlib>
using namespace metal;
kernel void bw_sum(device const uint4 *x [[buffer(0)]], device uint *out [[buffer(1)]], constant uint &n [[buffer(2)]],
                   uint tid [[thread_position_in_grid]], uint nt [[threads_per_grid]]) {
    uint4 acc = 0;
    for (uint i = tid; i < n; i += nt) acc ^= x[i];
    if ((acc.x ^ acc.y ^ acc.z ^ acc.w) == 0x12345678u) out[0] = 1;   // keep the loads
}
"""

from pathlib import Path
g = Path("dec_gpu.swift"); t = g.read_text()
anchor = '@_cdecl("dec_wait")'
t = t.replace(anchor, '''/// Steady-state timing (research): `iters` copies of the layer's work in ONE command buffer, no CPU gaps, so the GPU
/// stays at its loaded clock. Returns GPU ms per copy.
@_cdecl("dec_bench")
public func dec_bench(_ layer: Int32, _ h: UnsafePointer<Int32>, _ st: UnsafePointer<UInt32>, _ p0: Int32, _ iters: Int32) -> Double {
    let cb = queue.makeCommandBuffer()!
    for _ in 0..<Int(iters) { guard encode(cb, Int(layer), h, st, Int(p0)) else { return -1 } }
    cb.commit(); cb.waitUntilCompleted()
    if cb.status != .completed { return -2 }
    return (cb.gpuEndTime - cb.gpuStartTime) * 1e3 / Double(iters)
}
''' + anchor, 1)
g.write_text(t); print("ok")
EOF

    for (uint kb = k0; kb < k1; kb += SUB) {
        uint lb = kb - s0;
#if PF_MODE != 0
        for (int j = 0; j < 8; ++j) dummy ^= pfv[j];             // the previous tile's prefetch (completed during it)
        uint nb = kb + PF_DIST * SUB;
        if (nb < k1) {                                           // touch the tile PF_DIST ahead: every 128 B line once
            uint lbn = nb - s0;
#if PF_MODE & 1
            for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { uint L = t + j * NT; pfv[j] = *(device const uint *)(K + gh * 256 * R + (L >> 1) * R + lbn + (L & 1) * 128); }

// 8 x uint4 registers (loads issued right after the current tile is stored, so they overlap its compute); the QK / PV

114	## How we measured
115	
116	- **Prefill only**: driver timing from the first block to the last logits (`prototypes/v8_prefill.py --mode v8`).
117	- **Real end to end**: `--handover --ho-overlap`: the process also holds the whole target build. KV rows and DeltaNet
118	  states go to the ANE during the run, then 32 decode steps run on the ANE, teacher-forced against the ANE-only
119	  reference: decode KL and argmax agreement.
120	- **Server**: HTTP requests (`pipe/unit/srv_bench.py`, `srv_matrix.sh`), cold prompts plus follow-up turns.
121	- **Quality**: per-block last-token KL against the ANE-only reference (`--mode ref`) and the decode check. KL against
122	  the ANE-only path grows with length for every V8 variant (2K 0.010, 8K 0.020, 32K 0.030, 64K 0.058).
123	- **Thermal**: both units slow under sustained load with no pmset warning: the ANE FFN pair call goes from 2.6 to
124	  3.0-3.7 ms and the GPU mixer from 3.19 to 3.38 ms. A 5 min idle restores it. The first rep after a cool-down is
125	  about 5% fastest. Configs are compared only interleaved (A B A B) in one session, or rotated within one process.
126	- **Memory**: every long job runs under `tools/swap_watchdog.sh`. Weights must exist once (the GPU reads the ANE's
127	  compiled HWX zero-copy).
128	
129	## Variants, in order
130	
131	### V1: layer pipeline (hybrid), kept as the first baseline
132	
133	The ANE runs target chunks 0..c-1 of block b+1 while the GPU runs the remaining layers of block b, reading the weights
134	zero-copy from the ANE's compiled programs (`pipe/engine/gpu_layers.swift`, `prep_engine.py`). Best split 9 chunks on
135	the ANE, 28 layers on the GPU ("9/28"). Driver `prototypes/hybrid_prefill.py`, long context and decode handover
136	`prototypes/hybrid_long.py`.
137	
138	- 8K: 486 tok/s (ANE-only 303), 32K 412 (264), 64K 326 (217). KL vs ANE-only 0.0033 (2048 tokens). Decode after
139	  handover at 8K 32/32, KL 0.004.
140	- The GPU engine matches a float32 reference of the release math to 4e-4 (`pipe/ref_layers.py`).
141	- Splash 1.3.2 measured about the same: 2K 499, 8K 486, 32K 396-411, 64K 323-336.
142	
143	### V2 to V7: analysis and probes
144	
145	- **V2, attention offload to the GPU.** At 64K the ANE spends about 8.7 ms more per attention layer than at 8K. A GPU
146	  INT8 QK + PV would cost at least 3.1 ms at 64K. This needs ANE chunks split at the attention layers. Folded into V8.
147	- **V3 (mixers on the ANE, FFN on the GPU) and V4 (tensor parallel).** Rejected on arithmetic: GPU-bound, or a sync
148	  per matmul.
149	- **V6, GPU matmul units.** NAX (MPP matmul2d) runs at fp16 18, int8 x int8 30-33.6, uint8 32.8, fp8 e4m3 30-32, e5m2
150	  21, half x int8 19.4 and fp4 16.5 TOPS. simdgroup_matrix gives 4.8. FP8 needs Metal 4.1.
151	- **V7, splash-style W8A8 FFN** (runtime INT8 weights) in Core AI: about 10 TOPS, 2.4x slower than our compile-time
152	  LUT FFN. Rejected then. The A8 probe later showed the cause was the matmul shape, not the INT8 operands (see A8).
153	
154	### V8: every FFN on the ANE, every mixer on the GPU
155	
156	A per-layer comparative-advantage model gave the split. Per token per layer, the ANE LUT FFN costs 23.2 us against
157	36.3 on the GPU (1.56x better on the ANE), while the mixers cost about the same on both. The GPU mixes layer L of
158	block k once block k-1 has finished layer L and block k has finished the FFN of layer L-1. Buffers are the ANE's
159	IOSurfaces, used zero-copy.
160	
161	- **First prototype was slower than V1** (2K 470, 8K 445, 32K 363). Fixes:
162	  - a separate GPU waiter thread, so the submit thread never blocks;
163	  - more streams;
164	  - **pair mode**: one 128-row GPU call per two 64-row ANE blocks;
165	  - **one ANE thread per pair**: two threads calling the same Core AI function concurrently (different bindings)
166	    corrupt outputs at random (KL spikes 0.6 to 4).
167	  - Result: 2K 574, 8K 494, 32K 402.
168	- **ANE per-prediction gap.** Instruments shows a 0.23 ms idle gap before every ANE evaluation. It comes from the
169	  driver or firmware: private _ANEClient calls and Metal shared-event chaining (splash's method) show it too. Only
170	  fewer, bigger predictions help: `ffn2` entries run two 64-row blocks in one function, saving one gap per pair.
171	  Result: 2K 566-582, 8K 515.
172	- **Chunked FFN** (`v8/chunkmlp.py`, builder `V8_SPLIT=8192,9216`): the intermediate is split into two chunks of the
173	  same LUT palette. The ANE FFN takes 1.31-1.35 ms instead of 1.61-1.66 (-18%). Result: 2K 626-633 (+17%), 8K
174	  544-560, 32K 439.5. Other splits gained less ([9216, 8192] -10%, 4 to 17 chunks -5 to -14%) or lost
175	  ([16384, 1024] +24%).
176	  128-row ANE calls stay 19% worse per token than 64-row ones.
177	- **Shared-weight build** (`v8/build_shared_chunk.py`). Separate FFN-only and mixer builds duplicated every FFN weight
178	  and did not fit in 32 GB above 8K with the decode target loaded. One package per target chunk now carries the target
179	  entries (with the chunked FFN) and the V8 `ffn` / `ffn2` / `mix` / `mix2` entries on the same modules. Core AI
180	  stores each constant once, so a package is the size of the plain target chunk (593 to 703 MB).
181	- **Attention projections on the ANE** (`V8_ATTPROJ=1`, build v8_shared2):
182	  - `ffnq2_L` = FFN + the next attention layer's norm, rotation and q / k / v projections; `offn2_L` = o projection +
183	    residual + FFN. The GPU runs only the attention core (`v8_submit_attcore`).
184	  - Per attention layer per pair: ANE +1.28 ms, GPU -2.0 ms.
185	  - Helps where the GPU is the bound: 32K real e2e 490 vs 474.
186	  - Hurts below about 8K, where the ANE is the bound: 2K 594 vs 653, 6K 539 vs 592.
187	- **ANE mixers** (`mix2` entries for 16 DeltaNet layers, "M16"): +2.8% at 64K, +1.9% at 96K. Neutral to -20% at
188	  8K to 32K. Not used in the server.
189	- **Position-dependent offload** (attproj only past a position, `--attproj-from`): about 0.5% over "always".
190	- **GPU engine work** (`pipe/engine/gpu_v8.swift`, `kernels.metal`, env switches). Kept:
191	  - `CONC`: concurrent encoder, the recurrence overlaps z (-2% GPU time).
192	  - `FUSE=all`: fused norm / rotation / conv / gates / conversions.
193	  - `I8=1`: W8A8 DeltaNet LUT GEMMs, int8 palettes, activations int8 per 1024-ch block; KL unchanged.
194	  - `I8T=1` and `I8TV=_k256`: channel-major int8 tiles, K step 256.
195	  - `LUTC=1`: fp16 decode, K-column-fastest.
196	  - `I8A=1`: attention q / o in int8. `I8KV=1`: the export's int8 k / v weights. `LRM=1`: one low-rank B for qkv + z.
197	  - `QK8=1 TKV=2048`: int8 Q x K scores, 2048-key tiles.
198	  - Result: the DeltaNet layer per 128 rows went from 2.59 to 1.97 ms. 2K prefill-only reached 727 mean / 745 best;
199	    real e2e 2K 694 / 747, 6K 681 / 712.
200	- **Rejected engine variants** (still opt-in switches in the sources, outputs bit-same or correct):
201	  - Flash attention `FA2` (S in MPP cooperative tensors): correct but 11.2 ms vs 6.8 at 16K. Single-simdgroup
202	    matmul2d runs about 2x slower than the 4-simdgroup tiles.
203	  - Two-pass QK `QX`: exact, 7.52 vs 6.81 ms. Tile shapes (QK 64x128 / 128x64 / 128x128, PV 64x256 / 128x128): none
204	    beats 64x64 QK / 64x128 PV.
205	  - `SMF` softmax forms and `LRF` (low-rank folded into the GEMM): no gain.
206	  - `EPI` (residual in the epilogue): no gain under contention, decode KL worse.
207	  - `gdn_rec8` / `gdn_rec16`: slower; the recurrence is bound by its serial chain.
208	  - Double-buffered LUT tiles (`LUTV`), 256-row calls (`NTV=256`), int8 decode double buffering, `REC` / `LUTP`: no gain.
209	  - The GPU FFN for 2 to 6 layers (`I8F`, `--gpu-ffn`): the GPU becomes the bound, no gain.
210	- **KV8: the GPU attention reads and writes the ANE's INT8 KV segments.** No fp16 GPU KV, no KV copy, warm
211	  continuation possible.
212	  - The first version lost quality (decode KL 0.10 vs 0.02 at 16K). Cause: `attn_softmax8` put p x value-scale into
213	    fp16, wh

===== 2026-11-17T23:11:45.535Z
$ {"url": "https://engineering.drawthings.ai/p/llm-prefill-what-we-got-wrong", "prompt": "Summarize precisely: (1) what the article says about using the GPU and the Apple Neural Engine together during LLM prefill (how work is split: by rows/tokens of a matmul, by layer, by op type), (2) any measured numbers (tokens/s, utilization, speedups, which chips, which models, dense vs MoE), (3) what \"perfect overlap\" refers to, (4) anything about decode, (5) what they say they got wrong. Quote key sentences exactly where possible."}
----- output
**1. GPU and Apple Neural Engine split:** The article doesn't mention the Apple Neural Engine. It describes no split of prefill work across GPU and ANE, whether by matmul rows/tokens, by layer, or by op type.

**2. Measured numbers:**
- **M5 Ultra:** About 4,800 tokens/s for prompt processing (prefill).
- **M5 Max:** Expected at about 2,400 tokens/s "if it is truly compute-bound." This is a projection, not a measurement.
- **SSD streaming (model unnamed):** 160 GB of quantized weights, 10 GB/s effective SSD bandwidth, and about 38k tokens per pass to balance. With roughly 8k-token prompts, they estimate a 4–5× slowdown.
- **DeepSeek V4 Flash (MoE, 256 routed experts per layer, 43 MoE layers):**
  - 16 GiB config: about 75% of experts are activated per layer by around 3k tokens, where selective and full-layer loading become roughly equally fast.
  - 48 GiB warm config (3,072-token chunks, 40 streaming layers): about 190 experts selected per layer, about 90 already resident, so selective loading still wins.
- **Attention accuracy (LongBench v2, 60-case subset):** "Our INT8 QK + FP16 PV implementation came very close to Splash BF16." They note 39/60 vs. 40/60 is not statistically significant.
- **Kernel specialization overhead:** Usually 1–2% for larger GEMM and causal prefill attention, but more than 25% for FP16 attention with few query rows.
- No utilization figures or dense-model results are given.

**3. "Perfect overlap":** This refers to overlapping SSD weight reads with computation. Their 4–5× slowdown estimate assumes this overlap is perfect, and they still get that slowdown.

**4. Decode:** Only a brief mention. They say Local Code "still has amazing prefill and decode performance," but give no decode numbers or methods.

**5. What they got wrong:**
- They expected diffusion-engine experience to carry over to LLM prefill. "Our initial bet was that by leveraging our know-how in diffusion engines" was not borne out.
- SSD weight streaming is "fundamentally limited by SSD speed" for prefill, so offloading is not free unless prompts are very long.
- Low-precision attention that works for diffusion may hurt accuracy on long autoregressive contexts, so it needs more scrutiny.
- Shape-specialized kernels pay off poorly for irregular LLM sequence lengths, so they rewrote kernels to read dimensions at runtime.
